- Agregado gate consultivo de paridad de enumeración de gates a la revisión previa al push (`scripts/Test-PrePushGateEnumerationParity.ps1`): deriva del orquestador los gates realmente ejecutados y señala enumeraciones en la documentación de la rutina que quedaron como subconjunto propio; generaliza la regla de propagación a "conjunto enumerado" y cubre el agujero de dirección (enumeración que cita solo los miembros antiguos). Incluye self-test.
- Agregado gate consultivo de placeholder de trazabilidad a la revisión previa al push (`scripts/Test-PrePushHistoryCommitPlaceholder.ps1`): cuando el diff toca `historico/*.md`, señala el campo `Commit:`/`PR:` con placeholder genérico (`este commit`, `TODO`, etc.) en vez del hash real; integrado al orquestador como aviso, con self-test.
- Agregado gate consultivo de cobertura de skill transversal a la revisión previa al push (`scripts/Test-PrePushSharedScriptSkillCoverage.ps1`): cuando el diff altera un script compartido, lista los `SKILL.md`/`quality-checklist.md` que lo documentan y no fueron tocados; integrado al orquestador como aviso, con self-test.
- Agregado gate consultivo de propagación de término nuevo a la revisión previa al push (`scripts/Test-PrePushNewTokenPropagation.ps1`): cuando el diff introduce un parámetro/alias por transición colocalizada, lista menciones del repositorio que quedaron con el conjunto antiguo; integrado al orquestador como aviso, con self-test.
- Reforzada la fase semántica de la revisión previa al push para buscar términos nuevos agregados a contratos y reportar lagunas candidatas de trazabilidad separadas del veredicto.
- Estandarizados los wrappers compartidos de empaquetado, inventario y sanidad XPZ para JSON por defecto en stdout, sin `-AsJson`, con bloqueos estructurados y aliases operativos `-InputPath`/`-ObjectList`.
- Agregado gate para opciones costosas de build MSBuild: `CompileMains=true` y `DetailedNavigation=true` ahora exigen `-AllowCostlyBuildOptions` con confirmación explícita.
- Alineada la localización y trazabilidad de los documentos de gobernanza en `README.md`, `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md` e `historico/IdeiasImplementadas_202606.md`.
- Alineados `08-guia-para-agente-gpt.md`, `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md` y `999-ideias-pendentes.md` después de la nueva regla de changelog en la revisión previa al push.
- Incorporada a la rutina previa al push la evaluación semántica obligatoria de `CHANGELOG.md` para cambios con impacto público.
- Documentado en la guía de contribución que la revisión previa al push debe evaluar la actualización de `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Agregados documentos públicos de gobernanza: `SECURITY.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` y `CHANGELOG.md`.

## English

All relevant changes to this repository will be recorded here from this adoption onward. Earlier history remains distributed across `historico/`, commits, and work-front documentation.

The format keeps an `Unreleased` section for changes not yet published in a formal version. This repository does not yet use public semantic versioning; when that changes, future sections should record the corresponding tag.

### Unreleased

- **`WebPanelDesigner` / `SDPanelDesigner` disambiguation (exportable XPZ vs extension warning / SQL part):** after catalog promotion, the `999` entry that said K2BTools designer metadata «never appears in XPZ» gained a caveat (partial premise: `FormDesignerPart` remains SQL-only; the two settings types are exportable). `02` §providers gained a disambiguation rule and the classification trigger points at the effective catalog; `09`/`10` (`KB_Teste_Grande_A` evidence) note that the missing-extension warning is a distinct layer from the catalog type.

- **Shared catalog: seven K2BTools addon types:** promoted from a local override (KB `siawk603_P14web_GX18_UP15`, GeneXus 18 U15) into `scripts/gx-object-type-catalog.json` and `01a-catalogo-e-padroes-empiricos.md`: `K2BEntityServices`, `K2BPrompt`, `K2BTrnForm`, `K2BMenu`, `K2BGeneralSettings`, `WebPanelDesigner`, and `SDPanelDesigner`. All inventory-eligible with `queryableByKbIntelligence=false` (current extractor does not guarantee edges). `WebPanelDesigner`/`SDPanelDesigner` GUIDs match the SQL investigation in `999`. No KB-specific self-test fixture (Evo1 precedent in `998`). **Operational note (post-pull):** `CLEANUP_RECOMMENDED` only if each override entry is **operationally equivalent** to the base. The palliative registrar (`Register-GeneXusObjectTypeCatalogOverride.ps1`) writes `queryableByKbIntelligence=$true` by default; with the base at `false`, the typical divergence is `REMINDER_REQUIRED` / `field-divergence` until the entry is aligned or removed — do not treat cleanup as automatic merely because the GUID already landed upstream.

- **Shared catalog + legacy registry: `Menubar` (GUID `28eca899-…`):** the local override labeled the type `MicroInfo` from the description (`BbInfo` / `MICRO Info`); the IDE and the wiki [Menu Bar](https://docs.genexus.com/en/wiki?7610%2CMenu+Bar=) confirm **Menubar** (deprecated since GX15, Win generators). Promoted to the modern catalog (`folderName=Menubar`, `queryableByKbIntelligence=false`). The GX9 registry reclassifies `<Menubar>` from `orphan`/`gxlegacy/Menubar` to `equivalent`→`Menubar` (same pattern as `WorkPanel`), avoiding folder collision with the orphan and the disjointness self-test failure. Registry counts: `equivalent` 10→11, `orphan` 2→1 (`Report` only). Legacy export/inventory self-tests aligned. **Operational note (post-pull on the parallel folder):** while the override entry for that GUID diverges from the base on an identity field — typically `folderName=MicroInfo` versus `folderName=Menubar` — effective-catalog resolution **blocks** (`OVERRIDE_RESOLUTION_BLOCKED`, `unsafe-identity-divergence` / `identity-field-divergence`) until the entry is removed or identity-aligned to `Menubar`. Identity alignment guarantees **non-blocking**, not `CLEANUP_RECOMMENDED`: the engine compares all operational fields; advisory cleanup only when those are equivalent too (a divergent `queryableByKbIntelligence` becomes `REMINDER_REQUIRED` / `field-divergence`). The same criterion applies to the seven K2B override entries — full equivalence, not merely a promoted GUID. In parallel: corpora that already materialized `type=gxlegacy/Menubar` under a `Menubar/` folder need a legacy-package re-sync (or cleanup) before index rebuild.

- **Quota-pattern parity in `AntigravityCliSupport.ps1` (latent debt, not a live bug):** flagged by external review. The Antigravity support library's `$quotaFailurePattern` still had a bare `exhausted` — the same defect fixed in the dispatcher in `4f64c81` and never propagated to its namesake. The `\b(...)\b` gave no protection: «exhausted» is a whole word in `retries exhausted`, `connection pool exhausted`, and `context window exhausted`, which the Antigravity pattern classified as quota while the already-fixed dispatcher pattern said `error`. **Actual reach, measured before fixing:** that pattern is consumed **only** by its own self-test — `Invoke-Antigravity.ps1` uses `Get-AntigravityErrorMessage`, and the dispatcher runs the adapter in a separate process with its own pattern, so no scope collision is possible. So: an inconsistency and a latent trap (`09` and `SKILL.md` advertise the pattern as exported «for self-tests **and utilities**»), not a production failure. Aligned to the dispatcher pattern, with three **negative** cases in the self-test (9 → 19 cases in the file) validated by mutation: restoring the bare `exhausted` fails all three. Discarded in the same triage, as a testing convention rather than misleading prose: the dispatcher self-test's fixed counters (`reviewersDispatchAttempted -eq 4`) — an integration assert that breaks when backend #7 arrives is **doing its job**, a signal of a changed scenario, not silent drift.

- **Antigravity: an auth failure could become a «verdict» in the panel (engine parity with the Gemini fix):** flagged by external review. Two defects that compounded. **(1)** `AntigravityCliSupport.ps1` had a bare `\bauth\b` — the same defect fixed in `GeminiCliSupport.ps1` hours earlier, in the same workstream, and not propagated to backend #6: the extractor was **blind** to the interactive prompt `Opening authentication page in your browser...`. **(2)** `Invoke-Antigravity.ps1` falls back to **raw text** when stdout does not parse as JSON with exit 0, and returned that text **without consulting the extractor**. Together: a CLI that exits 0 and writes human text to stdout — exactly the state measured on Gemini CLI, same family, with no cached credentials — would have its login prompt returned **as the model's answer**; and since `responded` in the dispatcher is **mechanical** (non-empty text suffices), the auth failure would enter the panel receipt as a reviewer that **answered**, counting toward the diversity floor. Proven with a fake-exe before the fix: the adapter returned the login text with no error. The extractor now covers `authenticat*`/`authoriz*`/`sign in` (with `credential` deliberately left out, being an informational line in normal runs) and the fallback is **fail-closed** — known error matched ⇒ `BLOCK` with the reason; otherwise it still returns raw text, preserving resilience to CLI format changes. `Test-AntigravityCliSupportSelfTest.ps1` went from 9 to 14 cases covering both directions, and both fixes were **validated by mutation**: neutralizing the guard fails 2 cases; reverting the regex fails 3. The Achado D conclusion in `SKILL.md` gained the caveat that, on that fallback path, the text does not come from a named terminal field.

- **Third staleness class: numeric counts — fixed by removal (centralization level 1):** after name lists and script lists, external review exposed a class that **no search by backend name can find** — the word «four» does not contain «Antigravity». There were three: `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` said «in the **four** non-opencode» while the list right above already held five (`999` had been fixed to «five» hours earlier, inverting the divergence instead of eliminating it); and `scripts/Test-LlmDelegateStdinHandlingSelfTest.ps1` said «in the **6** adapters» with seven in the list, and «the **2** argument-based» with the **next line** already naming Gemini/Copilot/Antigravity. Fixed **by removing the count**, not by updating it: the text now refers to the list (`$messagePathAdapters`, «the non-opencode listed above»). And the count sweep — a class no earlier sweep covered — showed it was worth clearing **the still-correct ones too**, since a count that is right today is a time bomb at the next backend and removing it costs nothing: «There are **six** delegation engines» in the `SKILL.md` opening, «the **4** stdin-based» in the same self-test sentence, «in the **7** adapters» in `09` (the full list was already in the sentence), and «the **6** current backends» in the `mimo` entry in `999`. The `#1`..`#6` identifiers **stay**: they are each backend's stable identity, not a count of the set. Derived rule: **do not introduce backend/adapter counts in prose; refer to the list.** Out of scope as immutable records: `CHANGELOG`, `historico/`, and frozen design documents. One last count («nenhum dos **cinco**», in `999`) survived **two** cleanup passes and was found by external review — due to two flaws in the **search tool**, now recorded in `999` as input for level 3: (1) the script reported only the **first** match per line, and the line opened with «frente **dos 4** achados» (a proper name), which was triaged as a false positive and took the whole line with it; (2) the text was `nenhum dos **cinco**` and the regex required a space between the preposition and the number, without tolerating the **markdown bold** in between. Together with the line truncation that had already cost `09:114`, they are the same disease — **reducing the evidence before triaging it** — and a gate that repeats them produces a false negative that looks like a clean sweep. The `999` entry was rewritten as **centralizing the source of truth in three levels** (user decision): level 1 remove counts (done), level 2 pointer where it is pure duplication, level 3 a gate deriving from **code** — not from the skill's prose, which also rots, as this very case proved.

- **Four backend enumerations that never got #6 (found by external review, after the pre-push):** a reviewing agent flagged `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md:114` — the `Build-PrePushReviewDossier.ps1` pointer said `argv-limited` Copilot/Gemini, while the engine's own comment, `02`, `08`, `13`, and `14` already said Copilot/Gemini/Antigravity; a reader of the inventory would conclude that Antigravity is not subject to the ~30000-char ceiling nor to omission via `dossier-too-large` — precisely the backend most likely to blow past it. The follow-up sweep found three more, all in `999`: (1) the Achado D sweep origin claimed «does not reproduce in Codex/Claude Code/Gemini/Copilot» while `SKILL.md` already recorded Antigravity under the same conclusion — two documents disagreeing about the **same** sweep; (2) the «migrate to stdin» workstream listed only `Invoke-Gemini`/`Invoke-Copilot`, leaving out the third argument-based adapter, the one most sensitive to the ceiling; (3) the typed-output workstream omitted `Invoke-Antigravity` from the list of adapters to type. Discarded as weak or already correct: the 429-detection entry (Antigravity has partial coverage through `AntigravityCliSupport.ps1`'s `$quotaFailurePattern`), the illustrative «assume a subscription» mention in `SKILL.md`, the frozen `reviewer-ro` design document, and the self-test comments where the restricted list is correct (`enumeration=none-native` should indeed hold only copilot/gemini). The **root cause** was recorded in `999` as its own workstream: a consultative backend-enumeration parity gate, mirroring `Test-PrePushGateEnumerationParity.ps1` — which solves the same failure mode for the gate domain and whose own header records that «three reviews missed this». `newTokenPropagation` is blind to this case by construction: it looks for the **new** term, and the stale line does not contain it — which is precisely why it is stale.

- **Two gaps from the pre-push itself (`agy models` time-ceiling parity):** the pre-push over the final set showed that the timeout added to the Antigravity probe had landed only in the `CHANGELOG` and in the code comment — neither `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` (the normative owner) nor `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md` mentioned the ceiling or the degradation, so a reader would conclude that `enumeration=none-native` on Antigravity only means «no models», when it can now also mean «probe cut short by time». Both aligned. The second gap was coverage: the cut had no versioned guard (the proof was an ephemeral script), so a refactor could drop the `WaitForExit` with nothing to catch it. `Test-LlmDelegateCapabilityManifestSelfTest.ps1` gained case (I) — a hanging fake, the manifest must return anyway, degrading **only** Antigravity to `none-native` while the other backends stay enumerated. So the case does not cost the real ceiling, `Build-LlmDelegateCapabilityManifest.ps1` gained `-AntigravityProbeTimeoutSec` **TEST-ONLY** (default 20s), in the same convention as the dispatcher's test-only params. Validated by mutation: without the ceiling, the case fails with `levou 59.7s`.

- **Gemini: the auth diagnosis was being thrown away (detector layer 1):** `Invoke-Gemini.ps1` raised `BLOCK: ... JSON invalido` **from inside** the `ConvertFrom-Json` catch, before consulting `Get-GeminiErrorMessage` — which sits further down and would have found the real reason. In the state measured on 2026-08-06 (CLI **without** cached credentials), Gemini writes the interactive login prompt to **stdout** and exits **0**, so the adapter died with `Unexpected character encountered while parsing value: O` — the `O` from «Opening». The catch now consults the extractor first; only what it does not recognize is still reported as a broken JSON contract, and with a **stdout sample** attached. Alongside it, `Get-GeminiErrorMessage` now recognizes inflected forms: `\bauth\b` did **not** match «authentication»/«authenticating», precisely the words in both real failures (interactive prompt on stdout and `Error authenticating: IneligibleTierError` on stderr). `credential` was deliberately left **out** of the pattern — the CLI emits `Loaded cached credentials` during normal runs and that would inject noise into the error message. `Test-GeminiCliSupportSelfTest.ps1` gained behavioral fake-exe proofs (auth stdout ⇒ real reason and **not** «JSON invalido»; unreadable stdout ⇒ keeps the contract diagnosis, with a sample). The fate of backend #5 and detector layers 2 and 3 were recorded in `999`.

- **Real health dispatch per backend (2026-08-06) — two defects fixed:** the live-call test (trivial prompt, `public` payload, `allow` gate on all five) showed **opencode**, **Claude Code**, and **Copilot** answering, **Codex** at its usage limit (correct adapter behavior), and two real defects. **(1) Copilot with a rotten model default:** `Invoke-Copilot.ps1` pinned `$Model = 'gpt-5-mini'` and **always** passes `--model`, so the backend was broken **even when the caller omitted `-Model`** — Copilot CLI 1.0.78 answers `Model "gpt-5-mini" from --model flag is not available`. The adapter and `Resolve-CopilotModelLocality.ps1` defaults became **`auto`** (documented in `copilot --help`; Copilot picks the model), with destination key `github-copilot/auto` matching `github-copilot/*` like any concrete id; explicit models are still accepted. `Test-CopilotModelLocalitySelfTest.ps1` gained asserts for the default and the empty model, so a concrete id cannot come back as the default. Documentation parity in `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` (with a volatile-catalog note), `02`, `09`, and `14`. **(2) `agy models` probe with no time ceiling:** it was the **only** probe in `Build-LlmDelegateCapabilityManifest.ps1` that executes a CLI and waits on a network response (the others read config or merely check PATH presence), and without a timeout a hung `agy` — expired token falling into an interactive login prompt, service down — would hang the **entire** manifest, and with it the panel offer and the `xpz-kb-parallel-setup` snapshot. The risk stopped being theoretical the same day: Gemini CLI, unauthenticated, prints an interactive prompt instead of JSON. The probe moved to `Start-Process` + `WaitForExit(20s)` + `Kill`, with stdin closed and both pipes drained; on overflow it degrades to `enumeration=none-native`, the path the code already used when no models were found. Validated with a hanging fake: the manifest returns in 20.6s (previously, never), degrades only Antigravity, and leaves the other backends untouched; the happy path stays at ~2.5s.

- **`quota` classification regression in the older backends (introduced by Antigravity #6):** the 6th-backend commit widened `$quotaFailurePattern` in `scripts/Invoke-LlmDelegatePanelDispatch.ps1` with a **bare** `exhausted` term. Since that pattern classifies the state of **every** backend, any generic network or context error (`retries exhausted`, `connection pool exhausted`, `context window exhausted`) started being reported as `quota` in opencode, Codex, Copilot, and Gemini — and `SKILL.md` requires writing "bateu limite de uso" in the human receipt for that state, which would send the operator waiting for a cycle reset over a failure that is not a quota one (the mirror image of the 429-masked-as-timeout incident already recorded in this `CHANGELOG`). The term now requires the exhausted subject: `resource_exhausted` or `credits`/`balance`/`saldo` + `exhausted`; `quota exhausted` still matches through the `quota` term, and `429`/`too many requests` — the legitimate targets of the widening — remain intact. `Test-InvokeLlmDelegatePanelDispatchSelfTest.ps1` gained an end-to-end negative case (`retries exhausted` ⇒ `error`, `quotaCount=0`), validated by mutation: with the widened pattern the test fails. Claude Code's durable quota circuit was **not** affected (its state comes from the sidecar through `Convert-ClaudeCodeSidecarToPanelState`, not from this pattern).

- **Post-push Antigravity (#6) — 3 doc↔motor parity gaps fixed (identified by peer review):** (1) `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` (section «Fallback de curadoria v2») listed only `402` and `Payment Required` as quota signals, omitting `429`, `exhausted`, `resource_exhausted`, and `too many requests` that `Invoke-LlmDelegatePanelDispatch.ps1` had already been detecting since the Antigravity commit — a reader of the doc would conclude that HTTP 429 does not become `quota`, when today it does. (2) The confidentiality policy section enumerated Codex/Claude Code/Copilot/Gemini explaining that the adapter prefix does not appear in the policy key, but omitted Antigravity — the only backend whose policy key **uses** the adapter prefix (`antigravity/*`, not `google/*`); the gap was in confidentiality. (3) The header of `scripts/Test-LlmDelegateStdinHandlingSelfTest.ps1` (lines `:11`, `:19`, and `:189`) still described the argument-based regime as «`Invoke-Gemini`/`Invoke-Copilot`», even though the file body and `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md` already included `Invoke-Antigravity`. No motor behavior changes; all fixes are documentation and comments only.

- **Reinforced pre-push (round 2) — preferred reviewers, Antigravity self-test, and family in `15`:** `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` now includes `Antigravity` in the two preferred-reviewer prompt spots that still omitted backend #6. `Test-AntigravityCliSupportSelfTest.ps1` no longer fails the suite when `agy.exe` is not installed (case 4 becomes skip; fake-exe remains mandatory). Clarified that the Antigravity help contract is a **discovery minimum** (`-p`/`--mode`), not an enumeration of every adapter flag. `15-revisao-por-pares.md` tightens the family definition: key prefix, with special collapse only for `antigravity/*`.

- **Reinforced pre-push — Gemini inventory and structural family:** restored the `scripts/Resolve-GeminiModelLocality.ps1` entry in `09` (accidentally removed in commit `2e80a70` when inserting the 6th Antigravity backend). Aligned the `note` in `Resolve-LlmDelegatePanelDiversity.ps1` and the SYNOPSIS of `LlmDelegateTargetFamilySupport.ps1` with the structural-family definition (not “destination provider”). Documented in `Resolve-LlmDelegatePreferredReviewers.ps1` that `family` follows the shared helper (slash-less keys keep their own key).

- **Support for Antigravity CLI (`agy`) as the 6th Backend in the `xpz-llm-delegate` skill:** Added the Antigravity CLI (`agy -p`, backend #6) to the LLM delegation suite and peer-review panel (alongside opencode, Codex, Claude Code, Copilot, and Gemini). The new module `scripts/LlmDelegateTargetFamilySupport.ps1` (`Get-LlmDelegateTargetFamily`) dynamically resolves the structural model family (`antigravity/claude-*` $\rightarrow$ `anthropic`, `antigravity/gpt-*` $\rightarrow$ `openai`, `antigravity/gemini-*` or default $\rightarrow$ `google`), ensuring `antigravity/gemini-*` collapses into the `google` family (avoiding false diversity against the direct Gemini backend), while `antigravity/claude-*` scores as `anthropic` and slash-less/`unknown` keys keep their own family. The dispatcher `scripts/Invoke-LlmDelegatePanelDispatch.ps1` was updated to use `Get-LlmDelegateTargetFamily` at all 7 family-resolution points in the ledger, eliminating false family diversities in the panel receipt. The capability manifest `Build-LlmDelegateCapabilityManifest.ps1` fixed the Antigravity executable fallback to `Resolve-AntigravityExe -SkipContractCheck`, preventing `StrictMode` exceptions when `-AntigravityExe` is omitted in a real environment. Clarified the conceptualization of the model's structural family (distinct from the delivery provider) in `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` and `15-revisao-por-pares.md`. Updated the comment-based help of `Resolve-LlmDelegatePanelDiversity.ps1`, `Invoke-LlmDelegatePanelDispatch.ps1`, and the self-test suite (`Test-LlmDelegateAuthorizationSelfTest.ps1`, `Test-LlmDelegateCapabilityManifestSelfTest.ps1`). Documentation aligned in `README.md` (trilingual), `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md`, `xpz-skills-setup/SKILL.md`, `02`, `08`, `09`, and `13`. In `AGENTS.md` and in the mechanism/dossier-mode wording of `14`/`15`, the text was thinned to point to the skill (and to `13`); the illustrative panel composition in `14` still lists backends.

- **Support for Antigravity 2.0 in the `xpz-skills-setup` skill:** expanded inventory and audit engines to support Antigravity as the 5th managed agent tool (alongside Codex, Claude Code, Cursor, and OpenCode). The `scripts/Test-XpzSkillsRegistration.ps1` engine adds `Test-UsableCliOnPath` to ignore `WindowsApps` stubs, validates the global native path (`~/.gemini/config/skills/`), and deduplicates shared directories (`.agents/skills/`) via canonized `(Resolve-Path).Path`. The `scripts/Test-XpzGlobalInstructions.ps1` engine resolves Antigravity's global instructions source in precedence order (`.gemini/config/AGENTS.md` > `.gemini/config/GEMINI.md` > `.gemini/AGENTS.md` > `.gemini/GEMINI.md`) with `@` reference support. `scripts/Install-CursorGlobalInstructionsMcp.ps1` includes Antigravity in Cursor MCP's automatic fallback chain. Updated documentation and automated self-test suites.

- **Protection against global whitespace churn in import XML:** the 9-FD gate (`scripts/Test-GeneXusFrontAcervoDrift.ps1`) now adds partial mechanical protection in the `New-XpzImportPackage.ps1` flow: when there is a unique-GUID baseline, it detects strong removal of inherited trailing whitespace (`front-textual-fidelity-trim-removal-churn`) and blocks the package before the Python engine. `front-textual-fidelity-info` is informational only when there was a unique-GUID baseline but comparable UTF-8 text reading/decoding failed. The blocking payload includes `classification`, `whitespaceTrimPairCount`, `functionalChangedLineEstimate`, `acervoLineCount`, and `thresholds`; the informational payload uses `classification='not-applicable'` and `reason`, covered by self-test. `Copy-GeneXusAcervoToFront.ps1` now allows explicit-target rebuild even when the front is newer than the official corpus, so this textual block can be remediated without broad overwrites of the whole front; existing calls with `-ObjectList`, `-ObjectNames`, or `-ObjectGuids` now carry that rebuild/overwrite authorization for listed objects; an explicit target for an already existing/edited object may overwrite the local copy and must be deliberate. G4 in the parallel-folder pre-push now parses `git diff --no-renames --check` by structured path, distinguishing the official corpus, XML added in the front, and tracked/modified XML in the front, without turning unparsed lines into whitespace blocks by themselves. Compatibility: generic JSON consumers should treat G4 `gates[].detail` entries as structured objects, not strings; the human `-AsText` output renders those details as `path:line: message [classification=...]`. Explicit limit: `Build-GeneXusImportFileEnvelope.ps1` still covers `lastUpdate` and `Object/@type`, but does not cover strong global trim in this version. Because this front changes `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/SKILL.md` and examples covered by `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/setup-contract.manifest.json`, the setup contract signature changes and may trigger re-audit in parallel folders.
- **Procedure.Link in the KbIntelligence index:** `scripts/Build-KbIntelligenceIndex.py` now resolves `Procedure.Link(...)` in effective `Source`, `Property Formula`, and `WorkWithForWeb` action parameters, preserving the existing action-to-wrapper relation through `gxobject`. This closes false negatives in `who-uses` for indirect calls such as `procDisplayUrlSemPaginaEmbutida(<Procedure>.Link(...), ...)`; `EXTRACTOR_SIGNATURE_VERSION` `10→11`, with rebuild expected in parallel folders. The `Test-KbIntelligenceProcedureCallFormsSelfTest.ps1` self-test covers `Source`, `WebPanel` with `OpenNewWindow`, attribute formula, and action with wrapper.
- **Claude Code usage-limit reporting:** `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` now states that, for Claude Code, the `quota` state must be reported to humans only as a usage limit, with `tipo/período não determinado`. Raw CLI messages may remain in the technical ledger/diagnostic, but must not be converted into an inference of a monthly, weekly, 5-hour, or other billing-window limit in the human receipt.
- **Local wrapper inventory aligned with canonical templates:** `scripts/Test-XpzWrapperInventory.ps1` now reports `unsafe_last_exitcode_after_ps1_engine` generically for audited wrappers that call a PowerShell engine through a variable and still exit through `exit $LASTEXITCODE`, instead of restricting the check to `Register-*KbPostBuildEvents`. The equivalent canonical templates in `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/examples/` and the parallel-folder pre-push wrapper were updated to clear `$global:LASTEXITCODE` before invocation, capture `$?`, and resolve the exit code through `Get-Variable` with a 1/0 fallback. Because `Test-XpzWrapperInventory.ps1` and `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/examples/*` are part of `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/setup-contract.manifest.json`, the change alters the setup contract signature and may trigger re-audit in parallel folders. Documentation aligned in `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/SKILL.md` and `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md`.
- **Local governance for XPZ skills in this repository:** `AGENTS.md` was adjusted to allow operational use of XPZ skills and `xpz-llm-delegate` as mechanisms for this repository itself when there is an authorized human task, while keeping that use separate from critical evaluation of changes to skill contracts. The rule keeps the requirement to study documentation and request explicit approval before editing skill contracts, but removes the previous ban on using those skills as operational support in the repository that develops them.
- **Refined BuildAll/SpecifyGenerate post-build classification:** variable `TimeSpan` output and valid civil date/time output are now treated as strictly validated inert noise, while timing commands and the success marker still require an environment fingerprint. The shared stderr filter accepts only the known `anonymous`+`component` and `g_service_worker`+`obj` pairs, is also used during recovery and in headless wrappers that expose `stderrFilteredNoise`, and preserves real errors/crossed pairs. Deployment context access now supports `IDictionary` and `PSCustomObject` in both wrappers. Because `scripts/GeneXusKbDeploymentEnvironmentSupport.ps1` is also part of `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/setup-contract.manifest.json`, this part of the front alters the setup contract signature and may trigger re-audit in parallel folders. New self-tests cover the full flow, partial registration, negative controls, recovery, and SpecifyGenerate parity-by-reading; end-to-end parity for SpecifyGenerate post-build events remains tracked as follow-up.
- **Native textual editing route in Codex:** for already-approved textual changes in this root, `apply_patch` is again the normal route when available and not prohibited by a higher-precedence instruction. The `xpz-codex-apply-patch-alternative` skill and `scripts/Apply-ApprovedPatch.ps1` remain an auditable backup for concrete failures before any local write, native-tool unavailability, or explicit human instruction; the engine's earlier technical contract remains valid for that contingency. The skill now also states that, when globally available, the engine can operate on another target repository through `-RepositoryRoot`, without requiring a local script copy.

- **Claude Code asynchronous panel contract:** added `scripts/Invoke-ClaudeCodeAsync.ps1` so the dispatcher uses `stream-json` with an atomic technical sidecar (`claude-code-async-sidecar`, schema v1) and stdout containing only accepted final text (`resultAccepted=true`). `Invoke-LlmDelegatePanelDispatch.ps1` now emits `panel-summary.json` schema v2 even for structured failures, validates the sidecar before projecting states, exposes dispatch/process/sidecar/suppressed-fallback counters, and handles Claude quota through a durable base-key circuit (without `rateLimitType`), blocking before spawn/prompt, using a base-key half-open lease, and keeping `kb-sensitive` retention free of raw partial text. New/updated self-tests: `Test-ClaudeCodeAsyncSelfTest.ps1`, `Test-ClaudeCodeCliSupportSelfTest.ps1`, `Test-InvokeLlmDelegatePanelDispatchSelfTest.ps1`.

- Aligned the public contract for local XPZ catalog overrides: non-identity operational divergence remains a warning reminder with `REMINDER_REQUIRED` and warned merge, while identity divergence (`objectTypeGuid`, `rootKind`, `folderName`) blocks effective catalog resolution with `unsafe-identity-divergence`. The same rule is now reflected in `01a`, `02`, `09`, and the PowerShell/Python cores.
- Evolved the `gx-object-type-catalog.override.json` contract: reminder/audit now distinguishes `pending`, `redundant`, and `divergent` entries; equivalent redundancy returns `CLEANUP_RECOMMENDED`/exit 0 with `effectiveUpstreamPending=false`; effective merge preserves base operational fields missing from the override, including `exportTaskLabel`; local registration blocks redundancy before any write; parallel-folder setup must offer redundant cleanup in the consolidated plan, with approval, even when another mandatory item such as signature restamping is present. New self-test: `scripts/Test-XpzCatalogOverrideClassificationSelfTest.ps1`.
- **Mobile Work With registered by GUID and kept separate from the web type:** the global catalog now recognizes `WorkWith`/SD-mobile WW by GUID `15cf49b5-fc38-4899-91b5-395d02d79889`, folder `WorkWith`, and MSBuild label `WorkWithDevices`; `WorkWithForWeb` remains GUID `78cecefe-be7d-4980-86ce-8d6e91fba04b`, folder `WorkWithForWeb`, and label `WorkWith`. Current or historical pattern names do not classify the type: identity is always the GUID. During export preflight, an explicit `exportTaskLabel` takes precedence over canonical name/`folderName`; outside that context, canonical `WorkWith` remains mobile. KbIntelligence now indexes both types through the same extractors without collapsing same-named objects (`source_type` participates in deduplication); `EXTRACTOR_SIGNATURE_VERSION` `9→10`, with index rebuild expected in parallel folders. Real GxTest3 trials confirmed clean selective exports of one mobile and one web object; using a GUID as the MSBuild label was rejected and remains Category B.
- **Codex binary selection corrected:** `scripts/CodexCliSupport.ps1` now prefers the canonical `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenAI\Codex\bin\codex.exe` when usable, excludes `backup-*` directories, and only then falls back to the largest valid executable in a subdirectory. This supersedes the original strategy described below, which simply chose the highest version and could select an incompatible backup binary. `scripts/Test-CodexCliSupportSelfTest.ps1` covers canonical precedence, fallback, and blocking when only backups exist.
- **claude-code backend — false workspace-not-trusted alarm and three lying contracts:** the adapter classified every Claude Code failure as `workspace-not-trusted`, because it tested that detector before the generic filter and the CLI's `stderr` carries environment warnings that show up **even on successful runs** (measured 2026-07-25 with `claude 2.1.215`: identical `stderr`, byte for byte, on `exit 0` and on `exit 1`). Fixes: noise is stripped before any classification; new canonical code `max-turns-exhausted`; `workspace-not-trusted` is reserved for a genuine refusal, documented as a heuristic **never observed** — the CLI does not refuse to run, it merely discards the project's `permissions.allow` rules. Two derived defects were fixed in the same front: `-Tools ""` **omitted** the flag instead of disabling tools, inverting the documented effect (without `--tools` the CLI enables its full default set), and `-MaxTurns` was **silently discarded** because the adapters only sent `--max-turns` when the flag appeared in `--help`, which the CLI stopped advertising — parameter removed. A third derived defect was closed during the same front's pre-push review: the **asynchronous** path normalized nothing — `Watch-ClaudeCodeJob.ps1` watched only a `type=error` event, which the CLI does not emit on completion (measured with `claude 2.1.220`: the stream's last line is `type=result` carrying `subtype`/`is_error`), and `Resolve-ClaudeCodeJobStatus` returned the raw stream error; the watcher now recognizes `result` with `is_error=true`, and the stream error goes through the same detectors as the synchronous path, with `error_max_turns` recognized as turn exhaustion. Since turn exhaustion **may** arrive after some text has already been produced (measured both ways: a turn spent solely on `tool_use`, with no text, and a turn with a sentence emitted before the tool call), `result.json` gained `failureAfterText`: the final answer still wins (`status=completed`), but the later failure is no longer discarded — without that field, a truncated verdict would enter the panel as a good answer. Evidence extraction reads `subtype`, `terminal_reason`, `result`, and `errors[]`, because in the real `error_max_turns` event there is no `result` field and the human-readable message lives in `errors[]` — which the detector now recognizes as a third trigger, since `Reached maximum number of turns (N)` does not match the `text`-mode pattern. Aligned `scripts/ClaudeCodeCliSupport.ps1`, `scripts/Invoke-ClaudeCode.ps1`, `scripts/Start-ClaudeCodeJob.ps1`, `scripts/Watch-ClaudeCodeJob.ps1`, `scripts/Test-ClaudeCodeCliSupportSelfTest.ps1`, `scripts/Test-InvokeLlmDelegatePanelDispatchSelfTest.ps1`, `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md`, `09`, `998`, `999`, and `historico/IdeiasImplementadas_202607.md`. The conservative Claude Code exit-code glossary was also recorded: `0`/`1`/`2`/`137` come from specific official mentions, not from a general table, and the adapter must not infer quota, authentication, workspace, or model from the number alone.

- **API Object — OnlineShopSS functional listing:** the API from-spec front gained an incremental calibration based on the local OnlineShopSS test KB: an `API` collection variable with `AttCollection=True`, a listing Procedure using `For each` over a real Transaction/base table, an item SDT with `AttCollection=False`, dated coexistence of `idISBUSINESSCOMPONENT=True` with `idIsDynTrn=False`, and HTTP 200 as functional confirmation of the served endpoint without inferring GAM enforcement. Documentation was aligned in `xpz-builder`, `01e`, `02`, `03`, `08`, `10-base`, and the MSBuild skills; the scripted runtime gate remains future work in `999`.

- **API Object — OpenAPI YAML as complementary evidence:** the API from-spec front now records that OpenAPI YAML already generated by build/deploy can complement evidence of the observed public contract, as long as the file, environment, and freshness are declared. The YAML does not become a generation source, does not replace the official XML/XPZ, real import, build, HTTP smoke, or GAM validation, and `securitySchemes` remains only a public declaration: presence does not prove enforcement, and absence does not prove lack of internal configuration/authorization. Documentation aligned in `xpz-builder/responsibilities-by-type/api.md`, `01e`, `02`, `08`, `10-base`, `xpz-msbuild-build`, `998`, and `historico/IdeiasImplementadas_202607.md`.

- **Canonical approved-patch route for Codex:** the `xpz-codex-apply-patch-alternative` skill now explains newline-free Base64 delivery through the ProcessStartInfo route, that route's limits for multiple paths, the accepted textual format, and regression coverage for Base64, composite paths, CRLF, creation, deletion, and Windows short aliases. The engine accepts equivalent short/long `RepositoryRoot` spellings and adds Git diagnostics to preflight failures; exit codes and the stage/apply flow remain compatible.

- **Approved patch engine for Codex:** `scripts/Apply-ApprovedPatch.ps1` now stages a Base64 textual patch, binds its SHA-256 and exact paths before applying, and never commits, pushes, checks out, resets, or rolls back. New local skill: `xpz-codex-apply-patch-alternative`.

- **Procedure calls in the KbIntelligence index:** `scripts/Build-KbIntelligenceIndex.py` now resolves static `Procedure` call forms from the KB's real inventory, without depending on local naming prefixes such as `proc*`. Coverage includes `Nome(...)`, `Nome.Call(...)`, `Call(Nome, ...)`, `udp(Nome, ...)`, and `Nome.Udp(...)` in effective `Source`, `Attribute` `Property Formula`, and `WorkWithForWeb` conditions; dynamic calls (`Call(&Nome)`, `Call(ATT:Att)`, or quoted program names) remain outside the nominal graph. `EXTRACTOR_SIGNATURE_VERSION` 8→9; rebuild expected in parallel folders. New self-test: `scripts/Test-KbIntelligenceProcedureCallFormsSelfTest.ps1`.

- **9-BC Business Component sublevel gate:** `Test-GeneXusBCDependency.ps1` now recognizes sublevels declared in `<Level>` by either `name` or `type`, while keeping the same statuses and finding codes. The new `Test-GeneXusBCDependencySelfTest.ps1` covers valid BC dependency, multiple dependencies, sublevel by `name`, sublevel by `type`, missing sublevel, missing Transaction, false/absent `idISBUSINESSCOMPONENT`, and same-batch ordering risk.

- **Declarative mode in the `Source` sanity gate:** `Test-GeneXusSourceSanity.ps1` now recognizes only `Transaction` + `Rules` as declarative source and suppresses the false procedural findings `unexpected-close`, `mismatched-close`, and `unclosed-block`, while preserving warnings. Procedural behavior for `Events`, `Procedure`, all other pairs, and `Conditions` remains unchanged. The self-test covers the new pair, multiline `Conditions`, and the non-regression for `Procedure`.
- **Untrusted Claude Code workspace:** `xpz-llm-delegate` now classifies `stderr` that matches the heuristic untrusted-workspace detector as `unavailable`, rather than `error`, so the panel fallback chain can run. The detector uses word boundaries and does not inspect model `stdout`; the actual CLI message still requires empirical capture. The adapter preserves raw `stderr` and instructs the agent not to confirm trust automatically: it must ask the user for sanitized `stderr`, `claude --version`, and whether the refusal happened in Desktop or the CLI, or use another backend. Self-tests cover both classification and dispatch.
- **OpenCode 1.17.20 `reviewer-ro` compatibility:** the opencode adapters in `xpz-llm-delegate` now discover `opencode.exe` via override, `PATH` (WinGet/Scoop/direct binary), and legacy npm install, with validation through `opencode --version` in `OpenCodeCliSupport.ps1` and a dedicated self-test. Added `scripts/Test-OpenCodeReviewerRoInstalledCompatibility.ps1` to diagnose, without model/network access, when the guard blocks by version: it resolves the CLI, checks the `reviewer-ro` frontmatter, and verifies the effective allow-set from `opencode agent list`, separating `blocked`, `compatible`, and `needsFixtureRecapture`. The `reviewer-ro` fixtures were promoted to opencode 1.17.20; the `xpz-llm-delegate`, `08`, `09`, `999`, and least-privilege design documentation was aligned to also record internal `external_directory` exceptions and the urgent `.env`-inside-cwd risk.
- **Transactional preparation of peer-review artifacts:** added `scripts/New-LlmDelegatePeerReviewArtifacts.ps1` and a dedicated self-test to create `manuscript.md`, `reviewers.json`, and `preparation-manifest.json` as UTF-8 without BOM, through staging and atomic publication, from either `-ManuscriptText` or `-ManuscriptPath`. The `Invoke-LlmDelegatePanelDispatch.ps1` dispatcher now accepts exactly one of `-ManuscriptPath` and `-ManuscriptText`; in the inline path, it prepares artifacts before any gate/dispatch and, if preparation fails, emits its own `xpz-llm-panel-dispatch-result` with round/dispatch not started and zero reviewers dispatched. The success and failure summaries keep the same control shape (`success`, `roundStarted`, `dispatchStarted`, `reviewersDispatched`, `preparationError`). `-ManuscriptText` is reserved for short payloads; large payloads must use `-ManuscriptPath` to avoid the Windows command-line limit, and missing/ambiguous sources now return structured JSON. Documented in `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md`, `15-revisao-por-pares.md`, and `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md`.
- **MSBuild post-processing resilience:** `Invoke-GeneXusKbBuildAll.ps1` and `Invoke-GeneXusKbSpecifyGenerate.ps1` now initialize the state consumed by the outer `catch` before the `try`, preventing a second StrictMode failure when the flow fails early. `Test-GeneXusMsBuildPostProcessingResilienceSelfTest.ps1` also verifies that ordering, in addition to full-line filtering of known stderr noise.
- **External recovery without false success:** the BuildAll and SpecifyGenerate wrappers now preserve the prior classification, classified exit code, and `executionEvidence` when diagnostics fail after MSBuild. Degraded-completion literals require, respectively, `KbOpen` + `BuildAllDone` or `SpecifyDone` + `GenerateDone`; Category B, reorganization, timeout, non-zero MSBuild, or a missing marker cannot be converted into a clean result.
- **Accepted/rejected contract for the asynchronous opencode watcher:** `Watch-OpenCodeJob.ps1` now writes a local `schemaVersion=2` `<GUID>.result.json`, separating raw `finalText` from `acceptedFinalText` and exposing `resultAccepted`, `finalTextDisposition`, `rejectionReason`, `watcherExitCode`, `opencodeExitCode`, `hasStepFinish`, `completionVerdict`, and structured `fallbackDetail`. Fallback to `build`/default, truncation, missing completion, missing text, stream error, non-zero opencode exit, or unknown exit invalidates acceptance (`watcherExitCode=20`); internal watcher failure before atomic promotion exits with `99`, emits `WATCHER_INTERNAL_ERROR`, and does not promote result. Only the v2 contract validated by `Get-OpenCodeAcceptedResult` authorizes technical consumption: acceptance requires non-empty `acceptedFinalText` equal to `finalText`, `finishReason='stop'`, zero exits, `fallbackToBuild=false`, `fallbackDetail=null`, `finalTextDisposition='accepted'`, and `error=null`; rejected results with incoherent or fabricated internal fields are also refused by the helper. `Start-OpenCodeJob.ps1` now uses a minimal runner to write `<GUID>.exitcode.txt`; the initial `pid` is the `pwsh` runner, not the direct opencode PID, and the initial `result` field remains an expected path, not acceptance. Self-tests expanded in `Test-OpenCodeStreamSupportSelfTest.ps1` and `Test-OpenCodeReviewerRoSelfTest.ps1`.

- **Auditable fallback hardening for `xpz-llm-delegate`:** `preferred-reviewers.json` schema v2 now blocks invalid/duplicate `rank`, assigns implicit ranks after explicit ranks, documents/echoes `migratedFrom`, normalizes output by `rank`, limits `fallbackPolicy` to the contract actually supported by the dispatcher, cleans `.tmp-*` on atomic-write failure, and classifies recursive fallback timeout without leaving the child process alive or depending on raw `pwsh` from `PATH`. Closeout now requires the expected list; blocks missing, duplicate, or unmatched state for primaries and `fallbackChain[]`, both for preferred lists and explicit manual selections; echoes `expectedPreferredReviewerStates`; and points to `SelectedReviewersJson` when the expected list is missing. Focused self-tests updated in `Test-LlmDelegatePreferredReviewersSelfTest.ps1`, `Test-InvokeLlmDelegatePanelDispatchSelfTest.ps1`, and `Test-LlmDelegatePeerReviewCloseoutSelfTest.ps1`.
- **Auditable fallback in `xpz-llm-delegate` peer review:** the capability manifest now records sources and confidence without fabricating a provider (`targetModelKey`/`provider`/`family`/`sourceKind`/`sourceConfidence`/`availableInManifest`/`hardVeto`/`diagnostics`), including opencode JSONC, Codex without an invented prefix, and Claude settings/historical cache. `preferred-reviewers.json` migrated to schema v2 with primary reviewers ordered by `rank` and `fallbackChain[]` ordered `0..N`; the resolver preserves v1 and blocks `invokeArgs.backend` divergence, cycles, and hard veto. The dispatcher activates fallback in order for quota/timeout/error/unavailability, keeping `noResponse` as the orchestrator's post-hoc reclassification without automatically activating fallback, and records `skippedAfterSuccess`, `skippedByPolicy`, `notAttempted`, `attemptRole`, `fallbackOf`, and `countsForDiversity`; diversity and closeout block `insufficientDiversityAfterFallback`. Documented in `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md`, `15-revisao-por-pares.md`, `09`, and `999`. `xpz-llm-delegate` front self-tests updated and passing.
- **Java/Tomcat generator parity in XPZ skills — Java source and runtime-freshness Axes B/C:** recorded the 2026-07-09 read-only collection from the Java KB `EBTECH`, confirming the source root per environment (`<TargetFullPath>\web\src\main\java`), practical use of `JAVA_PACKAGE_NAME_FOLDER`, and GeneXus module mapping to subpackages. For Axis C, the evidence now treats `nav_objs.xml`, `ObjNavig`, navigation/specification XMLs, and local `.java`/`.class`/`.js` artifacts as signals to cross-check; the controlled experiment showed that `ObjStatus=genreq` is not an isolated Java freshness criterion. No script contract change; documentation adjusted in `evidence-catalog-java-tomcat.md`, `02`, `08`, `09`, `10-base`, `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/SKILL.md`, `xpz-msbuild-build/SKILL.md`, `xpz-msbuild-import-export/SKILL.md`, and the help for `scripts/Test-GeneXusRuntimeFreshness.ps1`.
- **Java/Tomcat generator parity in XPZ skills — opt-in metadata assistant:** added `scripts/Resolve-XpzJavaTomcatMetadataSuggestion.ps1`, a read-only assistant that cross-checks `model.ini` and `gradle.properties`, validates `WEB-INF\classes` topology, the `WEB-INF\lib\GeneXus.jar` sentinel, and the app package, and marks divergence as `needs-confirmation`. `Set-XpzKbSourceMetadataDeployment.ps1` now writes `kb_environment_servlet_dirs`/`kb_environment_app_package`/`kb_environment_servlet_flavor` only through explicit parameters; `metadata/deploy` validation now treats those fields as pending/blocking for `java-tomcat` KBs. Example wrappers, contract manifest, trilingual README, `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/SKILL.md`, and `09` aligned. Self-test: `Test-XpzJavaTomcatMetadataSuggestionSelfTest.ps1` (`XPZ_JAVA_TOMCAT_METADATA_SUGGESTION_SELFTEST_OK`).
- **Front-vs-acervo `Object/@type` drift block in XPZ packaging:** the 9-FD gate (`Test-GeneXusFrontAcervoDrift.ps1`) and the direct envelope builder (`Build-GeneXusImportFileEnvelope.ps1`) block when the front XML and the acervo share the same `guid` but diverge on `Object/@type` (`front-object-type-drift`), requiring a human decision instead of auto-copy/bump. Public type-drift contract migration: the new payload uses normalized `objectGuid`, `acervoPath`/`candidateAcervoPaths`, `acervoObjectType*`, and `message`; it does not use `frontGuid`, `guid` as the public type-drift key, or `baseline*`. Acervo duplicate GUIDs emit informational `front-object-type-drift-ambiguous-acervo`, without arbitrarily choosing an official type. `Copy-GeneXusAcervoToFront.ps1` resolves only temporal drift/seed and blocks auto-copy in that case; `New-XpzImportPackage.ps1` is the safe entry point, while `New-XpzImportPackage.py` is an internal engine. Helper `GeneXusObjectTypeDriftSupport.ps1` and self-tests cover the gate, front-based packaging, direct envelope assembly, and the auto-copy guard. Parity: `02`, `07`, `08`, `09`, trilingual README, `xpz-builder`, `xpz-msbuild-import-export`.
- **Java/Tomcat generator parity in the XPZ skills — Phase 5 (empirical validation):** recorded empirical evidence received from the Java KB `EBTECH` on a colleague's machine, without inferring results not measured on the dev machine (which has no Java license). The front documents: Jakarta/Tomcat 11/JDK 21 clean build with `Rebuild All -> Lf=∅, Pf≠∅`; observed latency between Gradle/publication and the last `.class`; copy actor `copyTomcat*`; conservative validation of suffixes `{_impl,__default,__gam}`; and measurement of the available GeneXus `JAVA_EE`/Gradle `javaEE` environment, with real `javax` signals on Tomcat 11/JDK 21/Servlet 6. The caveat is explicit: pure classic Java EE on Tomcat 8/9 + JDK 8 was **not** measured. No script contract change; separate follow-up to auto-populate Java metadata (`kb_environment_servlet_dirs`/`_app_package`/`_servlet_flavor`) through setup.
- **Java/Tomcat generator parity in the XPZ skills — Phase 3 (per-family deploy-bin gate engine, Axis A):** `java-tomcat` moved from `recognized-no-engine` (skip) to **`deployBinSupportState=supported`** — the deploy-bin gate runs a **per-family Java engine**, keeping Axes B (`.cs`/`.java`) and C (runtime) as `recognized-no-engine` (Post-v1). **PER-AXIS state split in the registry** `GeneXusKbHostingKindSupport.ps1`: `deployBinSupportState`/`sourceSupportState`/`runtimeSupportState` + predicates `runsDeployBinEngine`/`runsSourceEngine`/`runsRuntimeEngine` + `<axis>SkipStatus`/`<axis>UnsupportedReason`; each consumer migrated to its **own axis** field (deploy-bin policy/facade → A; `Resolve-GeneXusGeneratedCsPath.ps1` → B; `Test-GeneXusRuntimeFreshness.ps1` → C). **Migration-compat (breaking change of the output contract for Java KBs):** the legacy aliases `runsFreshnessEngine`/`freshnessSupportState`/`freshnessSkipStatus`/`unsupportedReason` **persist for 1 release cycle** (deprecated, derived from Axis A) for external consumers (parallel-folder wrappers, outside the reach of doc 13's sweep). **Engine** (`GeneXusKbDeployBinSupport.ps1`): `Get-GeneXusKbDeployBinPaths` became a **list** of targets (v1: 1 per KB); `Test-GeneXusKbDeployBinFreshnessCore` became a **per-family dispatcher** — `...CoreDotNet` (body extracted **with no behavior change**, regression anchor) and `...CoreJava` (new). The Java co-gate (`Get-GeneXusKbDeployBinJavaTarget` + `Get-GeneXusKbDeployBinJavaObjectMtimeMap`) checks **attested publication by the object's artifact set** (max mtime of the local `<obj>*.java` vs `<obj>*.class` in Tomcat's **external** `WEB-INF\classes`, grouping per object with **conditional strip** of the CLOSED set `{_impl,__default,__gam}`), **4-quadrant classification** (`fresh`/`stale`/`no-evidence`/`unexpected-publication`) + `unknown` (config-error), with a **BIDIRECTIONAL skew window** (a "future" `.class` under a Tomcat-ahead clock is excluded → **never a false `fresh`**; the OPERATIVE upper anchor in Phase 3 = `Now` — the public facade `Invoke-GeneXusKbDeployBinPostBuildClassification` does **not** wire `BuildEndedAt` (the design-preferred `DeployStepCompletedAt` is left for Phase 5), exposed in `deployBinCheck.thresholdSupAt` for observability via a StrictMode-safe guard; sensitive to clock skew between the KB machine and the external deploy server, resolved only in Phase 5). **Central safety property:** never emit `fresh` without attested publication in this build; every uncertain path fails conservative (`no-evidence` does not fail the gate; `stale`/`unexpected-publication`/`unknown` fail under gate, exit 49). External target resolved/validated by dedicated metadata (`kb_environment_servlet_dirs`/`_app_package`/`_servlet_flavor`; `WEB-INF\classes` topology + `WEB-INF\lib\GeneXus.jar` sentinel). **§9 no-bridge INVERTED** (denylist→**engine-file allowlist** + a drift guard of the allowlist itself). New self-test `scripts/Test-GeneXusDeployBinJavaCoGateSelfTest.ps1` (token `GENEXUS_DEPLOY_BIN_JAVA_COGATE_SELFTEST_OK`; 4 quadrants, stub-only, ahead/behind skew, topology, strip a/b/c/d, per-env `servletFlavor`, aliasing, cross-validation of `publicationTargets`, **`Now`/production branch** (safety-lock: a future `.class` is never `fresh` with `BuildEndedAt=$null` + `thresholdSupAt` sanity); fixtures via forged `LastWriteTime`, NTFS/ReFS). `.NET` golden recaptured (`java-tomcat` case = config-error `unknown` without `servlet_dirs`; the ACCEPTED/.NET and REJECTED cases **byte-for-byte identical** — .NET regression proven); routing self-test rewritten (java runs the engine). Frozen spec persisted in `java-tomcat-fase3-replano.md` + `java-tomcat-fase3-checklist-implementacao.md`. **Phase 5 (empirical) is NOT part of this front** — its own front, **gated by the colleague's Java KB** (the dev machine has no Java license; the fail-safe protects until grounding). Parity: `02`, `09`, `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/SKILL.md`, `999`. Design **frozen via peer review** of the replan (v1→v10, 5 families — anthropic/Opus, openai/gpt-5.5, deepseek-v4-pro, moonshot/kimi-k2.7-code, zhipu/glm-5.2; unanimous FREEZE/APPROVE, "proof transferred to implementation/self-test"). **Doc + scripts.** Origin: a Java/Tomcat KB user's proposal; Phase 0 assessed on a third party's machine.
- **Dossier-assisted mode in the reinforced pre-push review (semantic-only reviewer).** New `scripts/Build-PrePushReviewDossier.ps1` (+ self-test): the orchestrator runs the mechanical step and assembles a dossier — Section A (raw git) + Section B (`Invoke-PrePushMechanicalChecks.ps1 -AsJson`, labeled "candidates to verify, not truth"), with `HEAD:`/`END_DOSSIER sha256=` sentinels — delivered inline to the **semantic-only** reviewer (no shell: opencode `reviewer-ro`, Claude Code, Copilot, Gemini), which runs only the semantic phase; the **git-capable** reviewer (Codex-delegate, native subagent) is unchanged, and a valid panel keeps >=1 git-capable that reproduces Section A. Clean-tree gate before dispatching semantic-only. Documented in `13`/`14`/`15`/`02`/`08`/`09` and `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md`. Also: `scripts/Test-PyScriptsParse.ps1` hardened for a `scripts/` with no `.py` (no longer throws under StrictMode).
- **Local-wrapper-vs-template surface diff in the XPZ skills (new `surface_mismatch`/`INVENTORY_SURFACE_ADVISORY` check):** the parallel-folder wrapper inventory (`scripts/Test-XpzWrapperInventory.ps1` + helper `scripts/XpzWrapperEngineParamSupport.ps1`) now compares the **surface** {parameter names; `Mandatory`; the value set of each `[ValidateSet]`} of the local wrapper against the `.example.ps1` template, in the **opposite** direction to `forwards_unknown_engine_param` (there the wrapper forwards TOO MUCH to the engine; here the wrapper trails the template). New helpers `Get-XpzScriptParamSurface`/`Get-XpzWrapperSurfaceFinding` read the surface from the **top-level** `$ast.ParamBlock` (never a recursive `FindAll` — it must not inherit an inner function's `param()`). **Direction-aware severity:** it BLOCKS only the LOSS of a mandatory contract (`INVENTORY_CUSTOMIZED`, reason=`surface_mismatch`: template mandatory missing/downgraded, or no top-level `param()` while the template has a mandatory); it WARNS on optional/`[ValidateSet]` reductions (**new** label `INVENTORY_SURFACE_ADVISORY`, deliberately **outside** the aggregator's pendency regex — it does not block the setup state, same by-construction decision as `INVENTORY_ENGINE_DIAGNOSTIC`/`INVENTORY_RECOMMENDED_MISSING`); and it stays **quiet** on the surplus/ahead side (extra optional param, `[ValidateSet]` superset/removed, legitimate delegation to the engine — the template drops `[ValidateSet]` on purpose). `[ValidateSet]` is compared by **set difference** (catches a swapped value, not just count); multiple/`non-string`/mixed/empty = non-comparable (quiet). **Rollout:** blocks directly (small state flip — only mandatory loss blocks). New self-test in `scripts/Test-XpzWrapperInventorySelfTest.ps1` (unit + end-to-end + **regression guard** that locates the aggregator's pendency regex **by content** and fails if it matches the advisory labels) + inline comment in `scripts/Test-XpzSetupAudit.ps1`. Known limits (`[Alias]`, `ParameterSetName`, function/filter form, `DynamicParam`, type/default drift, `IgnoreCase=$false`) documented with severity in `999`. **Changes the `setup-contract.manifest.json` signature** (helper + inventory + `SKILL.md` are in the manifest) → triggers `AUDIT_REQUIRED` in parallel folders on next freshness. **Doc + scripts.** Parity: `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/SKILL.md`, `09`, `999`. - **Java/Tomcat generator parity in the XPZ skills — Phase 0 (empirical grounding) + Plan B triggered:** new `evidence-catalog-java-tomcat.md` records Phase 0 of the frozen design, with **real** evidence of GeneXus Java/Tomcat output (KB `EBTECH`, GX18U14, Jakarta flavor / Tomcat 10.1 / JDK 21) collected by an agent on a third party's machine (the dev machine **has no Java license**). **Verdict: EXTERNAL topology** — the served webapp is deployed exploded under Tomcat (`webapps\<app>\WEB-INF\classes`), **outside** the KB output tree, invalidating the *in-place* hypothesis the design froze for the Phase 3 engine and **triggering Plan B** (Plan B that would be implemented in Phase 3; the rest of the design — decisions (a)–(e), no-bridge clause — does **not** reopen). Grounding findings: deploy target locatable via the `model.ini` `SERVLET_DIR` (the Java analog of `kb_environment_web_dirs`); freshness = object `.class` under the external `WEB-INF\classes`, with **per-package** exclusion (`com.<kb>` kept, framework excluded), **not** by jar-name prefix as in .NET; sentinel `GeneXus.jar` (analog to `GxNetCoreStartup.dll`); **no `.war`** in the everyday flow (exploded deploy); **Gradle** build (GeneXus generation is content-aware: an identical-code no-op does not propagate); generated `.java` in local `web\src\main\java` ≠ external deploy `.class` (Axes A and B read distinct trees/volumes). **Q5b confirmed** (decisive re-test, manual IDE route, 2026-07-04): a real object change propagates **generation → Gradle recompile of `<obj>_impl.class` → deploy into the external `WEB-INF\classes`** (published = local); the freshness signal is measurable **by mtime** (a build with a change rewrites the object's `.class`; a no-change build touches nothing). Nuance: GeneXus generation is content-aware (a no-op does not propagate) and recompilation is **not** byte-deterministic → the gate must compare mtime-vs-build-start, not hash. **Deploy-target triage** (part C): `wsEducacaoSpTeste` = `e-mantém` (the .NET `NETFrameworkSQLServer` env stays the target; switching `deployment_hosting_kind=java-tomcat` would disable the .NET gate). The `JAVA_EE`/`javax.*` flavor (Tomcat 8/9) was **not** surveyed — the `java-tomcat` family must be namespace-agnostic. **Doc-only** — no script contract changed in this entry; the current Phase 3/5 state is recorded in later entries. Parity: `999-ideias-pendentes.md`. Origin: proposal from a Java/Tomcat KB user; empirical collection on a colleague's machine.
- **Opencode reviewer least-privilege «no execution/writes» (default BREAKING CHANGE):** the `Invoke-OpenCode.ps1`/`Start-OpenCodeJob.ps1` adapters now use **default `-Agent reviewer-ro`** (previously: no `-Agent` = full-access `build` agent) + a **fail-closed guard** in the adapter itself (`scripts/OpenCodeReviewerRoGuard.ps1`, dot-sourced): **static** pre-check (frontmatter) + `opencode agent list` confirming the **resolved allow-set is EXACTLY `{read,grep,glob,list}`** (locks both absence AND excess) + **tested version**, all **BEFORE** the run/spawn; any failure ⇒ **BLOCK** with a reason (`static`/`version`/`allowset`/`agentlist`-transient-SQLite). An **explicit** `-Agent <x>` = conscious opt-out (agentic use outside the panel; only confirms `<x>` resolves, avoiding the silent fallback to `build`). The panel blocks the `agent` key → the reviewer **always** falls back to `reviewer-ro`. Closes execution/writes (`bash`/`edit`) and the network tools (`webfetch`/`websearch`/`task`); `external_directory: deny` blocks reads outside the inherited cwd by default, without proving absolute isolation — the old premise «`read` reads any file on the machine» is **refuted by measurement** (opencode 1.4.4; `permission: deny` ≡ `tools: false`). Provisioning: `.opencode/agent/reviewer-ro.md` (versioned project-local; **cascading** exception in `.gitignore` — only the agent file, not the runtime state) + `scripts/Install-OpenCodeReviewerRoAgent.ps1` (global, **localized JSONC** edit preserving comments, migrates the interim `tools:`→`permission`) + versioned fixtures in `xpz-llm-delegate/fixtures/opencode-reviewer-ro/`. - **Cross-flow origin divergence (`dataSource`) detection in materialization — central modern↔legacy case (GeneXus 9 export):** `scripts/Sync-GeneXusXpzToXml.ps1` now detects, before writing metadata/XML, when an incoming item collides in the repository (`FolderType|NormalizedName`) with a file of a **different origin** — the real case is a repository that received both a **modern** export (no `dataSource`) and a **legacy** one (`dataSource="gx-legacy-export"` + `<GxLegacyPayload>` + `guid=""`), where the second silently overwrote the first by `lastUpdate`. New function `Get-GeneXusCrossFlowDataSourceCollisions` (compares the incoming node's `dataSource` with that of the already-materialized file, encoding-aware parse via `XmlDocument.Load`; **Decision E.2** — when the attribute is absent, the direct child `<GxLegacyPayload>` signals legacy, `guid=""` only corroborates) and helper `Resolve-ItemDestinationPath` (single path reused by `Write-ItemToDestination`). **Default fail-soft posture:** new field `Summary.CrossFlowCollisions` (array; `[]` when empty) on stdout + per-item boolean `Writes[].CrossFlowCollision` in the report (`-ReportPath`); every collision also goes to **stderr** as JSONL (`CROSSFLOW_COLLISION: {…}`), and parse warnings as `CROSSFLOW_WARNING: {…}`. **Opt-in block:** new switch `-BlockCrossFlowDataSource` aborts the sync (terminating error `ErrorId=CrossFlowDataSourceCollisionBlocked`, preceded by the stable line `CROSSFLOW_BLOCKED_ERRORID:` on stderr) **before** any metadata/XML — separate from the mixed-package fail-closed. **`SchemaVersion` stays `1`** (additive fields; consumer sweep: the only template that consumes the sync JSON, `xpz-kb-parallel-setup/examples/Update-KbFromXpz.example.ps1`, reads by name via `PSObject.Properties` and tolerates new fields). **Intentional behavior change (normative caveat):** the **modern** branch was reordered to `catalog → unknown-throw → Convert → detection → metadata` — from this entry on, the **unknown-type** and **intra-package collision** fail-closeds abort **before** writing `kb-source-metadata.md` (previously they wrote metadata and only then failed). The **atomicity asymmetry** remains and is declared: the **rename** fail-closed under `-FullSnapshot` (`Resolve-GuidAwareRenames`) still aborts **after** metadata (pre-existing, out of scope). **Residual risk (Decision G):** for a **non-parseable** existing XML, detection **neither collides nor blocks** (not even under `-Block`), only a warning — but it **does not guarantee the overwrite**: the sync may fail further on in pre-existing parse stages (`Get-LastUpdateInfoFromFile`/`Test-PackageMaterialization`), out of this front's scope; documented in `01k`. **Residual limitation (Decision E):** a legacy file deposited **with no signal at all** (`dataSource`/`<GxLegacyPayload>`/`guid=""`) is treated as modern and does not trigger a collision (marginal — the legacy engine always stamps it). New self-test `scripts/Test-XpzSyncCrossFlowCollisionSelfTest.ps1` (token `GENEXUS_XPZ_SYNC_CROSSFLOW_SELFTEST_OK`; child process; covers fail-soft, `-Block` with metadata atomicity, `-VerifyOnly`, E.2, non-parseable, and the per-item boolean). - Added the **fail-safe Phase 2a cleanup executor** to `xpz-kb-parallel-pre-push`: `scripts/Remove-XpzKbFrenteHygieneFindings.ps1` evolves Phase 2a from diagnosis-only to diagnosis + remediation. It consumes the JSON from `Test-XpzKbFrenteHygiene.ps1` as the **single source of truth** (it does not reimplement the package↔front matching) and removes only what was flagged (`frentesNaoConformes`/`pacotesOrfaos`; `pacotesNaoPadronizados` is **never** touched, mirrored in `untouchedNonStandard`). **Fail-safe:** dry-run by default (`-WhatIf` too), real deletion only with `-Apply`; `-Scope Frentes|Pacotes|Ambos`. **Safety:** it anchors `frentesDir`/`pacotesDir` under the canonical `-RepoRoot` and refuses a reparse point (junction/symlink) on any path component or item descendant; directory removal is **bottom-up** (never `-Recurse`); reparse is re-checked right before the operation. **Cascade:** under `-Apply` it re-invokes the engine each pass and **iterates to a fixed point** (removing fronts orphans sibling `Nome_DATA_NN` packages), with a `-MaxPasses` fuse (default 5); termination by finite-universe consumption + fuse (without claiming monotonicity). **Optional backup** `-Backup <dir> -RunStamp <id>`: same-volume move-aside, outside the target folders, with `backup-manifest.json` (cross-volume refused in v1 — follow-up in `999`). **Contract:** 1-line JSON (`Kind=xpz-frente-hygiene-cleanup-result`/`SchemaVersion=1`), human text on stderr only; the normative `status` (`clean`/`findings`/`applied-clean`/`applied-with-skips`/`not-stabilized`/`error`) is authoritative; exit `0/2/3/1` (automation reads `status`, not the number — exit 2 coincides with the engine's `warn`). **Producer breaking change** (same work-front, lockstep): `Test-XpzKbFrenteHygiene.ps1` now emits `Kind`/`SchemaVersion` + resolved `frentesDir`/`pacotesDir`; a captured `-JsonPath` requires those fields (it refuses JSON without `Kind` or from another `repoRoot`/dir). The only consumer of the JSON was the engine's self-test (sweep confirmed). New self-test `scripts/Test-XpzKbFrenteHygieneCleanupSelfTest.ps1` (token `XPZ_KB_FRENTE_HYGIENE_CLEANUP_SELFTEST_OK`): dry-run doesn't delete, `-Apply`, selectivity, cascade, reparse refused, JSON without Kind/divergent → error, stale JSON → skip, idempotency, non-standard untouched, backup (happy path + guards). New `## Quando o design estabiliza: congelar o papel e migrar para o código` subsection in `15`: when the **architecture** stops being reopened and the remaining objections are about **implementation precision** (not design gaps), the agent **proposes** and the **human decides** to freeze the paper and transfer the proof to implementation/self-test — recorded in the closeout as `resubmissionDeclinedByHuman` (reason "proof transferred"), **not** `resubmitted`; **freezing ≠ converging** (the strong sense of the rule stays reserved), and the live rule (stale/dissenter/whole panel) and panel composition (`999`) stay **unchanged** (orthogonal axes: how many versions × how many reviewers). New **"Regressão auto-infligida (churn do autor)"** bullet in the rule: diff vN+1 against vN on the closed decisions to flag self-inflicted regression in a large-context session. **Paper vs code** note in the `15` relationship table. Pointer in `14`; reflected in the `08:122` bullet; example of the **two subcases** of `resubmissionDeclinedByHuman` (freeze × abandonment) in the `SKILL.md` closeout. **Doc-only.** Design converged via **peer review** (v1→v4, multi-family panel: native anthropic, openai/Codex gpt-5.5, ollama-cloud deepseek-v4-pro/kimi-k2.7-code; glm-5.2 unavailable due to timeout/quota); human freeze recorded (reason "proof transferred to the diff + pre-push"). The round itself validated the work-front live: v4 (a marginal round) flagged an author churn the author introduced in v3.
- `xpz-llm-delegate` + `AGENTS.md`: documented the **canonical invocation form for the delegation adapters** (residue (b1) of the `999` URGENT item). New `## Forma canônica de invocação dos adapters` section in `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` (at the end of the `## SCRIPTS` block) and new `## Invocação dos adapters de delegação` section in `AGENTS.md` (+ a parity bullet in «Alinhamento entre documentos»), so the convention lives where **every** agent reads (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, OpenCode). Rule: **atomic** command, prompt always via `-MessagePath <file>`, zero embedded quotes; **form per tool** — Bash with a **relative** path (`pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/<Adapter>.ps1 …`, matches `Bash(pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/*)`) and a PowerShell fallback with an **absolute** path (`& "<abs>\scripts\<Adapter>.ps1" …`, matches `PowerShell(& "<repo>\scripts\*" *)`); the `&` call-operator with a relative path is **not** covered. Sync `Invoke-*` vs existing `Start-*Job` jobs (`-TimeoutSec` only on the sync ones); `-Cd` only on the adapters that support it (opencode does **not**); ~32KB caveat (cross-ref): `-MessagePath` removes the inline command substitution at the caller for all, but only the stdin-based ones escape the ceiling — argument-based (Gemini/Copilot) keep the ceiling with a 30000-char guard. Includes the real 2026-06-21 incident with the correct cause (embedded quotes breaking the literal match, **same** PowerShell family, not a tool mismatch) and a non-normative allowlist-pattern note. The allowlist (b2) is **local** housekeeping (git-ignored), optional — **(b1) removes the drift at the source**. Plan converged via **peer review** (v1→v4, multi-family panel: native anthropic, openai/Codex gpt-5.5, ollama-cloud deepseek-v4-pro/glm-5.2). Migrated to `historico/IdeiasImplementadas_202606.md`; the `999` URGENT entry shrank to residue (b2).
- Added the peer-review **panel dispatch harness** to `xpz-llm-delegate`: `scripts/Invoke-LlmDelegatePanelDispatch.ps1` mechanizes the previously ad-hoc **dispatch+collect** — it is the «dispatch harness» foreseen as future in `15-revisao-por-pares.md:108`. Per reviewer (input `[{backend, targetModelKey, invokeArgs, family?}]`): it resolves the **effective model** + fail-closeds (opencode = `invokeArgs.model` or the **input** `targetModelKey`, since the opencode resolver requires `-Model`; codex = `invokeArgs.model` or gate **without** `-Model` → last segment of the returned `targetModelKey`; claude-code/copilot/gemini with no `model` → `error`), runs the gate `Resolve-LlmDelegateAuthorization.ps1` **without authorizing** (`allow`→dispatch, `ask`/`deny`→`gateAsk`/`gateDeny`, throw→`error`+`gateVerdict=null`) and dispatches the `allow` ones to the `Invoke-*.ps1` adapters **in parallel** (`ForEach-Object -Parallel -ThrottleLimit 8` + `[SemaphoreSlim]::new($OllamaConcurrency)` via `$using:` **only** for `ollama-cloud/*`; `$collected` captured **before** `Dispose`; **decision I-a**: NOT `ThreadJob`, absent on this machine). **Structural** classification (no prose parsing): non-empty text→`responded` (**mechanical**; the off-task→`noResponse` reclassification is the orchestrator's post-hoc step, `15:65`), quota→`unavailable`, timeout→`timeout`, rest→`error`. Containment (`permissionMode`/`tools`/`maxTurns`/`agent`/`approvalMode!=plan`) **refused** into `securityBlockedArgs` and **not** forwarded to the adapter — dispatch proceeds with the safe defaults (**Position B**, a security decision); `approvalMode=plan`→silent `droppedArgs`. opencode in `kb-sensitive`→`unavailable` (**public-only in v1**; confinement deferred); `-Cd` precedence (explicit→`ParallelKbRoot` when sensitive→cwd) + **fail-closed** (opencode never gets `-Cd`); `-MaxAttempts 2` opencode only. **single-flight DEFERRED** (decision II-b): a concurrent failure→`error`+raw ledger+`concurrencySaturationWarning` on stderr. **Output:** stdout = **1 line** `panel-summary.json` (`Kind=xpz-llm-panel-dispatch-result`/`SchemaVersion=1` PascalCase, UTF-8 no BOM; human text via `[Console]::Error`; the caller captures stdout/stderr **separately**) + `manifest.json` + a per-state ledger (`.verdict`/`.error`/`.state`) under `<TempDir>/<RoundId>/`. It does **NOT** inject the native subagent, the floor, closeout, triage, convergence, nor refactor the 6 adapters (orchestrator / 999 fronts). TEST-ONLY params: `-OpenCodeConfigPath`/`-CodexConfigPath`/`-BackendExeMap` (fake-exe on the REAL adapter + gate driven by synthetic config/policy). New self-test `scripts/Test-InvokeLlmDelegatePanelDispatchSelfTest.ps1` (token `OK: Test-InvokeLlmDelegatePanelDispatchSelfTest.ps1`): covers effective model + fail-closeds, codex gate with `oss`, a THROWING gate, opencode-kb→unavailable, splat+containment, parallelism (occupancy ≤`OllamaConcurrency` for `ollama-cloud/*`; others free; a throwing block doesn't abort; `OllamaConcurrency=0`→validation), no single-flight (fake NOT re-invoked + `concurrencySaturationWarning`), mechanical classification, `-Cd` precedence+fail-closed, and the contract (1 line, accents intact, state subset, empty `targetModelKey`→null, Windows-safe slug, missing `RoundId`→guid, `ReviewersJson` inline/file/invalid). Documented in `09-inventario-e-rastreabilidade-publica.md`, `xpz-llm-delegate/SKILL.md` (SCRIPTS + the «Harness de disparo do painel» section with the orchestrator↔harness split and the decisions) and `15-revisao-por-pares.md` (harness from future→implemented). **Design converged via peer review** (v11, whole panel 4 APPROVE / zero gaps, 3 families — native anthropic, openai/Codex gpt-5.5, ollama-cloud deepseek-v4-pro+kimi-k2.7-code —, including the dissenting Codex, which found the v10 P1 gap of the opencode model circularity and re-approved on v11).
- `xpz-llm-delegate`: extended **`-MessagePath`** (load the prompt from a **file**; exclusive `Inline`/`FromFile` ParameterSet; `Get-Content -LiteralPath -Raw -Encoding utf8` + a `Test-Path -LiteralPath -PathType Leaf` guard) to the remaining delegation adapters — `Invoke-Codex.ps1`, `Start-CodexJob.ps1`, `Invoke-ClaudeCode.ps1`, `Start-ClaudeCodeJob.ps1` (stdin-based) and `Invoke-Gemini.ps1`, `Invoke-Copilot.ps1` (argument-based) —, mirroring `Invoke-OpenCode.ps1`/`Start-OpenCodeJob.ps1` which already had it. **Reason (root problem suffered for months):** dispatching an adapter with a large prompt required `-Message (Get-Content -Raw '<file>')` inline → a **compound** command → the harness allowlist (which matches **atomic** commands) raised an authorization prompt every time; `-MessagePath` makes the dispatch **atomic**. **Additive** change, default unchanged. For the **stdin-based** adapters, `-MessagePath` only changes the **origin** of the text (the prompt already went via stdin, immune to the ~32KB Windows command-line ceiling; in the `Start-*Job` scripts, `$Message` is populated **before** building `request.json`/`stdin.txt`, where the text stays persisted as today). For the **argument-based** ones (Gemini/Copilot), the prompt still goes in the **argv** via the runner → `-MessagePath` only resolves the caller friction and does **NOT** raise the ~32KB ceiling; hence they gained a **fail-closed size guard** (`$MaxArgvPromptChars = 30000`, **heuristic in chars** — UTF-16 code units, a conservative margin under the physical ~32767 ceiling, covering both `-Message` and `-MessagePath`) that rejects large prompts with `BLOCK` before `Argument list too long`. The real Gemini/Copilot migration to **stdin** remains a follow-up (blocked by lack of a subscription to validate empirically) in `999`.
